If they ask

Is the citizen view just the same page with buttons hidden?
No. The evidence list is filtered in the API: GET /api/cases/31/evidence returns 5 rows for the admin token and 1 row for the citizen token — I measured both. GET /api/cases/31/timeline returns 200 for staff and 403 "Staff only" for the citizen. POST /cases/31/evidence with a citizen token is 403 "Insufficient permissions".
Can an officer close a case?
No, and it is enforced twice: the Close button is not rendered for role=officer, and POST /api/cases/31/close with an officer token returns 403. Only lead and admin can close (require_role("lead")).
Where does the citizen satisfaction rating show up?
Honest answer: only as an aggregate. Ratings are stored per case (5 ratings: 4,4,5,3,5) and surface as the Dashboard KPI "Avg rating 4.2/5 (5)". The case workspace never shows the rating a case received — for staff there is no rating element at all, and for the citizen the star widget is an input that always starts empty. Do not promise "open a closed case and see its rating" on stage; it is not there.
How do you assign a case to an officer?
Today, via the API only: POST /api/cases/{id}/assign {"user_ids":[19]} (lead+ only, 25ms, writes an assign_case audit row and notifies the assignee). There is no button for it in the UI. Cases inherit the creator's team at creation, which is how the officer sees them at all.
Is the timeline a real audit log or a rendering of the case fields?
Real table. audit_log rows are written by the API on create_case, close_case, add_evidence, citizen_visibility, assign_case, rate_case, triage_complaint, exports and admin actions, each with actor_id and timestamp; the case Timeline tab is a filtered view of it and the Admin → security tab shows the same log unfiltered.
Why do the seeded cases only have two timeline entries?
Because the seed writes only the two audit events that actually happened (created, closed). Everything you do live appends properly — my test case ended with 4 entries after add_evidence, citizen_visibility and close. If you want a fat timeline on stage, add evidence and flip visibility before opening the tab.
A case converted from a complaint has no 'case created' entry in its timeline — why?
Correct, and it is a gap. The triage endpoint audits triage_complaint against the complaint entity, not create_case against the new case, so a converted case's audit trail starts at its first post-creation action. The Complaints screen still links the two ("View case #38").
What happens if two officers edit the same case at once?
Nothing stops them — there is no optimistic locking or field-level versioning. Evidence and messages are append-only so they merge fine; close/visibility are last-write-wins and both writes land in the audit log.

Careful / known limits

  • Case #31's Evidence tab now contains a 5th row captioned "rbac probe" (kind=note, visibility=team, by Const. A. Patel, dated today) and its timeline has three entries dated today. I created these while proving the API enforces roles, and there is no delete endpoint for evidence or audit rows — an admin has to remove them in SQLite (DELETE FROM evidence WHERE case_id=31 AND caption='rbac probe'). Simplest fix for the demo: open case #33 instead.
  • There is a 10th case, #38 "Online fraud reported" (Critical, priority 1, closed, citizen-visible), created by my complaint→case conversion test. It sits at the TOP of the case list because the list sorts by created_at DESC. Harmless and realistic, but the presenter should know it is not seed data.
  • No case shows its citizen rating anywhere. If the script says "here is the 4-star rating this case earned", that line has to change — the number only exists on the Dashboard as an average.
  • No assignment UI at all. Do not say "and I assign it to Constable Patel" while pointing at the screen; there is no control to click.
  • Severity, priority and status cannot be edited after a case exists (except closing it). If a judge asks to escalate a case from High to Critical live, it cannot be done from the UI.
  • Documents tab is empty on all 10 cases and shows no empty-state message — just a bare form. Skip it unless you type a FIR live.
  • Schedule tab is empty on all 10 cases. Only open it if you are going to book a meeting during the demo.
  • When the API rate limit (120 req/min, shared) trips, tabs render as empty rather than erroring: I measured the Timeline tab showing "No activity." on case #33 while the API had 2 entries. Keep other browser tabs and background pollers closed during the demo.
  • Silent no-ops: empty evidence caption, empty verdict, and empty new-case title all do nothing with no message.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • 10 cases total — 4 active, 6 closed (9 before my conversion test: 4 active / 5 closed)
  • 28 seeded evidence rows across 9 cases: 4 on each closed case, 2 on each active case (30 now, including my 2 test rows)
  • 23 case messages across 9 cases (2–3 per case)
  • Case #33 (recommended stage case): 4 evidence, 3 messages, 2 timeline entries, 5★ rating
  • Cases list rendered in 2.57s from nav click to 9 cards
  • Case close (verdict write + audit + re-render): ~2s in the UI
  • POST /api/cases/{id}/assign: 200 in 25ms
  • 5 citizen ratings stored, average 4.2/5 (Dashboard KPI); distribution 4,4,5,3,5
  • Zero JavaScript page errors and zero non-429 API errors across every case screen tested

Complaints

The 2001-row complaint inbox and the triage modal that turns any complaint into a live case in one click — powerful conversion flow, but the list itself has no search, no filters and no pagination.

Get there: Sign in as admin@city.gov / admin123 → top nav "Complaints". (As citizen the same nav item shows "My complaints" plus the intake form.)

Show this

  1. Complaint → case in one modal: pick severity and priority, type the response the citizen will be emailed, tick "Open a case from this complaint", Confirm — and you land inside a brand-new case workspace with the complaint's title, description and complainant already attached.
    Do: Complaints → any row → "Triage & open case" → Severity=Critical, Priority=1, response message, checkbox ON → Confirm.
    This is the closed loop judges look for: citizen report → officer triage → case file → verdict → citizen sees it. The complaint row then flips to "Converted" with a "View case #38" button that jumps straight back into the case.
  2. The cyber-fraud intake is a real NCRP-shaped form, not a text box: 10 fraud categories, 8 fraud channels, 30 Ahmedabad areas, amount lost, hours since incident — and if hours ≤ 24 a red "Golden hour: call 1930 immediately — fraudulent transfers may still be freezable" banner fires, with the applicable legal sections and a prevention tip printed underneath.
    Do: Sign in as citizen@example.com / citizen123 → Complaints → tick "Cybercrime" → Fraud type = UPI / OTP Fraud → Amount 250000 → Hours since incident = 2.
    It shows domain knowledge a generic complaint form does not have, and the golden-hour rule is the single most operationally useful thing on the screen for Ahmedabad Cyber Crime Branch.
  3. Volume: 2001 complaints, each rendered with its cyber category, ₹ amount lost, fraud channel and Ahmedabad area — the same corpus that drives the City Map and the analytics.
    It makes the platform look like a station's actual inbox rather than a demo with six rows. 1.06 MB of JSON serves in ~50ms.

What NOT to promise"Let me search the complaints for chain snatching in Maninagar"There is no search or filter on this screen at all. If you need a subset, use the City Map / analytics screens which do filter by area and category.

If they ask

2001 complaints and no search box — how does an officer find one?
Today they cannot, from this screen. The endpoint returns the whole table with no limit/offset/query parameters and the UI renders every row. In practice the filtering lives on the City Map and analytics screens (by area, category, severity). Adding ?q=&status=&limit= to GET /api/complaints plus an input on this page is the obvious next commit.
Does rendering 2001 rows not kill the browser?
Measured, not guessed: 2.79s to first paint, all 2001 rows in the DOM, 2.54s to scroll to the bottom, no crash, 1.06 MB payload in ~50ms. It works — it just will not scale to a real station's 200k complaints without pagination.
Can a citizen see other citizens' complaints?
The query is WHERE citizen_id = ?, so no. But be careful how you phrase it on stage: in this demo database all 2001 synthetic complaints are owned by the one demo citizen account, so the citizen login shows 2001 rows. That is seed design, not a leak.
What actually happens when you tick 'Open a case from this complaint'?
One transaction: the complaint is updated (severity, priority, response, status='assigned'), a case row is inserted carrying the complaint's title, description, complainant id and the chosen severity/priority, then the complaint is flipped to status='converted' with case_id set. The citizen gets a notification with the response message; the assigned team gets one too.
Is the complaint→case link bidirectional?
Yes. The complaint row gains "View case #N", and the case row stores complaint_id. The only gap is the audit trail: conversion is logged as triage_complaint against the complaint, so the new case's own timeline has no create_case entry.
Who can triage?
lead and admin. Measured: POST /api/complaints/{id}/triage returns 403 "Insufficient permissions" for the officer and the citizen token; the button is not rendered for them either.
Where did 2001 complaints come from — is this real data?
Synthetic, deterministic, and honestly labelled: the seed derives area-wise distributions from public NCRB/press reporting (docs/AHMEDABAD_CRIME_DATA.md) and spreads them over the last ~6 months (2026-02-20 to 2026-08-19). Category mix: assault 492, theft 318, vehicle_theft 301, cyber_fraud 273, body_offences 251, chain_snatching 214, murder 61, burglary 47, narcotics 41.

Careful / known limits

  • No search, no category filter, no status filter, no date filter, no pagination, no sort control — the task asked me to exercise them and they do not exist. Do not build a demo beat around finding a specific complaint.
  • No complaint detail view. Clicking a row does nothing; everything the system knows is already on the card. If a judge says "open that one", there is nothing to open.
  • Two complaints (ids 13914, 13918) are now status='assigned' from my triage-timing tests, and one is 'converted' into case #38. Additive only; 386 of 2001 were already 'assigned'.
  • When the API rate limit trips (429), the cyber-category labels fall back to raw keys — a row reads "loan_app_extortion" instead of "Loan-App Extortion". Cosmetic, but it is what a rate-limited screen looks like.
  • The triage modal's Confirm takes ~4s of visible spinner even though the API answers in 22ms, because the screen re-fetches and re-renders all 2001 complaint rows afterwards. Warn the presenter so they do not double-click (a second click would triage again).
  • Citizen complaint list is also unpaginated — the citizen portal loads all 2001 of their own complaints.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • 2001 complaints in the inbox; 1,060,914 bytes of JSON served in 40–57ms (3 runs)
  • 2.79s from nav click to first rendered row; all 2001 rows in the DOM; 2.54s to scroll to the bottom
  • Status mix: submitted 807, under_review 798, assigned 386, converted 10
  • Category mix: assault 492, theft 318, vehicle_theft 301, cyber_fraud 273, body_offences 251, chain_snatching 214, murder 61, burglary 47, narcotics 41
  • Date range spanned: 2026-02-20 to 2026-08-19 (~6 months)
  • Triage API: 22ms with a citizen response message, 17ms without; complaint→case in the UI ~4s including the full list re-render
  • Triage buttons rendered: 1992 for admin, 1991 for lead, 0 for officer, 0 for citizen
  • Intake taxonomy: 10 cyber-fraud categories, 8 fraud channels, 30 Ahmedabad areas

18 of 27

Citizen portal — public-facing complaint flow

A public complaint front-door with a cyber-fraud-specific form, live status and police reply visible to the reporter, a deep link into their own case, and a star rating once it closes — with the police-internal half of the platform enforced away at the API, not just hidden in the nav.

Get there: Sign in as citizen@example.com / citizen123 → lands directly on Complaints ("My Complaints"). Nav is only Legal Feed / Cases / Complaints.

Show this

  1. Tick 'Cybercrime' and the form transforms into a fraud intake: 10 named fraud types, 8 fraud channels, amount lost in ₹, and 'Hours ago?'. Enter a low number of hours and a red banner appears: 'Golden hour: call 1930 immediately — fraudulent transfers may still be freezable.'
    Do: Complaints → tick 'Cybercrime' → Fraud type 'UPI / OTP Fraud' → Amount lost 47500 → Hours ago 3 → the golden-hour banner appears above Submit.
    This is domain knowledge, not a generic form. The 1930 golden-hour window is the actual thing that decides whether a victim's money is recoverable, and the product nudges the citizen toward it at the exact moment they are typing the amount they lost.
  2. Submit and the complaint appears instantly at the top of the citizen's own list with a live status chip — Submitted → Under review → Assigned → Converted — and, where police have replied, the reply text inline: 'Police response: FIR registered; 1930 freeze request raised with the bank nodal officer.'
    Do: Fill the form → Submit complaint → your complaint is row 1 with a 'Submitted' chip. Scroll one row down to the seeded 'Online fraud reported' to show a 'Converted' complaint with a police response and a 'View case #38' button.
    Closes the loop that is missing from almost every real government complaint portal. The citizen sees the same status the station sees, plus the actual words the officer wrote, without a phone call.
  3. Click 'View case #38' and the citizen is inside their own case — status Closed, severity Critical, and the verdict: 'Amount frozen at the beneficiary bank and refunded; chargesheet filed under BNS 318(4) and IT Act 66D.' Then five stars: 'Rate your experience'.
    Do: On the 'Converted' complaint click 'View case #38' → read the verdict → click the 4th star → 'Thanks for rating (4★).' → switch to admin and show AVG RATING on the Dashboard.
    A citizen-facing outcome with a real legal disposition, and a feedback loop that feeds the station's own dashboard — the Command Dashboard shows AVG RATING 4.17/5 from 6 ratings, which is the citizens' score of the police, computed from this exact widget.
  4. The citizen sees 6 of the 10 cases. The other 4 are not merely hidden — requesting them directly returns 403, and their evidence returns 403.
    Do: If challenged: a citizen token on GET /api/cases/29 returns 403, /api/cases/29/evidence returns 403, and /api/analytics/summary, /api/analytics/map, /api/anomalies and /api/predict/risk all return 403.
    The answer to the obvious security question is a status code, not a screenshot. Visibility is a per-case citizen_visible flag enforced server-side; the nav hiding is cosmetic on top of a real boundary.
  5. Inside their own closed case the citizen gets exactly two tabs — Evidence and Messages — and the Evidence tab says 'No evidence visible' even though the case carries 1 evidence item.
    Do: Open case #38 as the citizen → point out only Evidence and Messages exist (no Notes, Documents, Timeline or Schedule) → open Evidence → 'No evidence visible.'
    Evidence is gated per item, not per case. The citizen can be told a case is closed and why, without ever seeing the CCTV frames or the investigator's notes that got it there.

If they ask

How do you stop a citizen from seeing police-internal case data?
Two layers, and the one that matters is server-side. Cases carry a citizen_visible flag; the citizen sees 6 of 10 and a direct GET on any of the other 4 returns 403, as does their evidence. Staff analytics — /analytics/summary, /analytics/map, /anomalies, /predict/risk — all return 403 for a citizen token. Inside a case they can see, they get two tabs (Evidence, Messages) instead of the staff set, and evidence is gated per item: case #38 has 1 evidence record and the citizen sees 'No evidence visible.' Hiding the nav is just cosmetics on top of that.
Your demo citizen sees 2002 complaints. Isn't that a data leak?
Fair challenge, and I chased it. It is a seeding artefact, not a leak: the synthetic seed attributed all 2002 complaints to citizen_id 20, which is the demo citizen, so they genuinely are the reporter of every one. I proved the filter works by registering a second citizen — that account saw 0 complaints, filed one, then saw exactly 1. Scoping is correct; the demo account is just an unusually prolific victim.
What is the 'golden hour' banner actually doing?
It's a client-side rule on the 'Hours ago?' field. If the fraud is recent it surfaces the 1930 advisory, because within that window the transfer can often still be frozen at the beneficiary bank. It's a prompt, not an integration — we are not calling 1930 or the bank. The seeded case verdict shows what the follow-through looks like when a station does act on it: 'Amount frozen at the beneficiary bank and refunded.'
Can a citizen rate any case, or spam ratings?
The widget only renders for a non-staff user on a case with status 'closed' that is visible to them, and POST /cases/{id}/rate is scoped to that. What I would flag honestly: the client calls rateCase with .catch(() => {}) and then unconditionally shows 'Thanks for rating' — so if the write ever failed the citizen would be told it succeeded. I did not find a duplicate-rating guard on the client either.
Why can a citizen list all 16 CCTV feeds?
That's the weakest point I found on this screen. GET /api/videos returns 200 for a citizen token with camera IDs, filenames, fps, durations and keyframe counts. It is metadata only — no frames, no thumbnails, no search, and the frame and search endpoints are gated — but a citizen learning that CAM-14 is 'traffic_redcar_06.mp4' is more than they need. It reads like the video routes predate the platform's RBAC layer and were never brought under it.
Does the citizen see the officer's real name and notes?
No. They see the case status, severity, priority, a member count, and the verdict text an officer wrote when closing. Investigation notes, documents, the timeline and the schedule are staff-only tabs that are not rendered for them, and evidence is per-item gated. The 'Police response' on the complaint is a field an officer deliberately fills during triage — it's meant to be read by the citizen.
What happens between 'Submitted' and a case existing?
A lead or admin opens the complaint in the staff inbox and triages it: sets severity and priority, writes the response message the citizen will see, and ticks 'Open a case'. That converts the complaint (status becomes 'Converted'), creates the case, and the citizen's complaint card grows a 'View case #N' button. All four statuses — Submitted, Under review, Assigned, Converted — are visible to the citizen throughout.
Is the complaint form validated on the server or just the browser?
Both, but not symmetrically. Title and description are HTML5-required in the browser, and the server rejects malformed payloads with a 422. The uncomfortable part is what the front end does with a 422 — see the login page notes; the same array-shaped error body that crashes the registration form would crash other screens too. The complaint submit path itself does not use that error-rendering pattern, so it is safe.

Careful / known limits

  • GET /api/videos returns 200 for a citizen token, exposing all 16 camera IDs, source filenames, fps, durations and keyframe counts. Metadata only — frames and search are gated — but it is not access-controlled the way the analytics endpoints are.
  • Rating is a silent-success path: CaseWorkspace.jsx:348 calls API.rateCase(...).catch(() => {}) and then unconditionally sets done, so a failed write still shows 'Thanks for rating (N★).' There is also no visible guard against rating the same case twice from the UI.
  • The staff complaint inbox renders all 2002 complaints in one list — 29,022 DOM nodes and 1,993 'Triage' buttons, no pagination, no search, no filter. It renders in 1.7s and uses 17.4 MB of heap so it is not broken, but do not scroll it looking for a specific complaint on stage; you will scroll for a long time.
  • The citizen case header shows '· 1 member(s)' — the size of the investigating team. Harmless but it is internal staffing information appearing on a citizen-facing screen.
  • Complaint titles and descriptions in the seeded data are English only; the categories, statuses and severities around them translate. In a Hindi or Gujarati demo the complaint cards therefore look half-translated. That is seed data, not an i18n bug, but it looks like one.
  • Registering a new citizen with a password shorter than 6 characters crashes the entire app to the error screen — see the Login page for the full write-up. It is reachable from the citizen portal's own 'New citizen? Register' link, so it belongs on this page's do-not-touch list too.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • 2003 complaints in the system at test time (2002 before I filed one; the Dashboard COMPLAINTS tile read 2003)
  • 10 cases total: 4 active, 6 closed. A citizen sees 6; the other 4 return 403
  • 10 cyber fraud types, 8 fraud channels, 20+ Ahmedabad areas in the complaint form
  • 4 complaint statuses visible to the citizen: Submitted, Under review, Assigned, Converted
  • Complaint submit → appears in the citizen's list: immediate, 0 page errors, 0 HTTP 4xx
  • Rating: POST /api/cases/38/rate → 200; station AVG RATING moved to 4.17/5 from 6 ratings
  • Second-citizen scoping test: 0 complaints visible before filing, exactly 1 after
  • Citizen token vs staff endpoints: 403 on /analytics/summary, /analytics/map, /anomalies, /predict/risk
  • Staff complaint inbox: 29,022 DOM nodes, 1,993 triage buttons, 1.7s render, 17.4 MB JS heap, no pagination
  • 8 helplines in the footer, tap-to-call: 1930, 112, 100, 1091, 1098, 14567, 108, 101

Citizen portal (citizen@example.com)

The other half of the closed loop, from the citizen's side — a three-tab product with an NCRP-shaped cyber intake, a read-only case view, and the officer's own response text delivered back as a notification.

Get there: Sign out → sign in as citizen@example.com / citizen123. Nav collapses to three items: Legal Feed / Cases / Complaints. "Complaints" is the landing page and shows the intake form above "My Complaints".

Show this

  1. Same account, same URL, radically smaller product: 3 nav items instead of 10, 2 case tabs instead of 5, no toggles, no Close button, no VisionScan and no Admin.
    Do: Second browser → citizen@example.com / citizen123 → Cases → open the same case the admin has open. Count the tabs on each screen.
    Run it in a second browser window beside the admin session. The RBAC is not CSS — the case evidence list is filtered server-side, and staff-only endpoints return 403 to this token. Two windows, one truth, different views.
  2. The citizen's notification feed reads back the investigating officer's own words: "Update on your complaint 'Online fraud reported': FIR registered; 1930 freeze request raised with the bank nodal officer."
    Do: Admin: triage a complaint with a response message. Citizen window: open the bell. The text is there.
    That sentence was typed by an officer in the triage modal on the staff side. Showing the citizen bell immediately after doing a triage on the admin side is the single tightest demonstration of the closed loop in the whole platform.
  3. The citizen sees 6 cases and 12 notifications — including six separate "Your case has been closed. You may now rate your experience." prompts — so the satisfaction rating that drives the dashboard's AVG RATING tile has a visible origin.
    It closes the metric loop: the 4.17/5 on the command dashboard is not a seeded constant, it comes from these prompts.

If they ask

Is the citizen view just the staff view with things hidden by CSS?
No. The case evidence list is filtered in the API — the same case returns 5 rows to an admin token and 1 row to the citizen token — and the staff-only endpoints answer 403 to this token, not 200-with-hidden-content. The nav difference is a consequence of the role, and the server would refuse the requests anyway.
Why does the citizen see 6 cases when there are 10?
Because case visibility to the complainant is an explicit per-case toggle an officer sets, not a default. Four cases have not been shared. That toggle also writes an audit row, so there is a record of who exposed what to whom and when.
Can a citizen spam the investigating team with messages?
No — the API caps it at one message per case per citizen and returns 403 'Citizens may send only one message per case' on the second attempt. It is enforced server-side, not by disabling a button.
Where does the 4.17/5 satisfaction score on the command dashboard come from?
From these accounts. When a case closes, the citizen gets a 'You may now rate your experience' notification and a star widget appears on the closed case. Six ratings have been given, averaging 4.17. The honest gap is that the rating is only ever shown back as a station-wide average — no case screen displays the rating that individual case received.

Careful / known limits

  • The two newest complaints in the citizen's own list are QA artefacts: "QA probe scope test" (newest in the entire database, 2026-08-19 20:03:36 UTC) and "QA UPI OTP fraud 013203". They are the FIRST two rows the citizen sees. They are also the first two rows the admin sees on the staff Complaints inbox. If the demo script says 'let me triage the top complaint', the top complaint is a QA row.
  • Case #38 — the one closed case a citizen can open with a full verdict — was created by a QA agent's complaint-to-case conversion test, not by the seed. It looks completely realistic; the presenter should just not describe it as pre-existing station data.
  • The citizen's complaint list is unpaginated like the staff one: every complaint owned by the account renders into the DOM. Because all 2,003 synthetic complaints belong to this single demo citizen, the citizen portal loads the entire city's synthetic backlog. It works, but do not scroll it and do not call it 'my complaints' in a way that invites counting.
  • The citizen advisory notification is the QA test broadcast — '[MEDIUM] QA test advisory — ignore: Pre-demo check of the broadcast pipeline. Deactivated immediately.' It is second from the top of the citizen bell.
  • The citizen star-rating widget is an input only — it never shows the rating already given, so re-opening a rated case looks like it was never rated.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • Citizen nav: 3 items (vs 10 for admin). Citizen case tabs: 2 (vs 5 for staff)
  • 6 cases visible to the citizen out of 10 total
  • 12 notifications in the citizen bell across 5 event types; 6 of them are case-closed rating prompts
  • 30 Ahmedabad areas in the intake dropdown

Admin — the two buttons that end the demo (security tab)

Correction and extension of a page whose report was truncated: the security tab contains the most impressive admin feature and the single most demo-ending button, and one of them has no confirmation.

Get there: Admin → security. Also covers the data tab, which the coverage document I was handed cuts off mid-sentence.

Show this

  1. Emergency lockdown: "When active, only administrators can operate the platform — every other request is rejected (HTTP 423)." It has an inline two-step confirm before it can be enabled.
    Do: Admin → security. Point at the lockdown card and the current state chip ("normal operation"). Click "Activate lockdown" ONLY if you want to show the confirm appear — then click the × to cancel. Never confirm.
    A named HTTP status for a policy state, with a guard in front of it. It is the right answer to "what do you do during a breach" and the confirm step is evidence that someone thought about the operator, not just the feature.
  2. The login-attempt table is live evidence for the platform's biggest technical caveat, on screen: every row reads IP 172.20.0.3.
    Do: Admin → security → "Recent login attempts". Point at the IP column.
    That is the nginx container's address, not any browser's. It is a one-glance proof of why the rate limiter treats all clients as one, and it turns a weakness into a technically literate observation the presenter volunteers first.
  3. The data tab's four exports each state their own integrity property: "WAL-safe SQLite snapshot of the entire platform database", and "Every download is recorded in the audit log."
    WAL-safe is the specific detail that tells a DBA this was not a naive file copy of a live SQLite database.

Exact inputs

FieldValue — click to copyWhat you should get
Best admin tab to open on stagemonitoring"Requests 12,349 · Errors (5xx) 0" beside three loaded models and an Operational badge. It answers the reliability question in one screen and there is nothing dangerous to click on it
Second bestdataFour exports. Downloading "Full backup (.zip)" then flipping to security to show the fresh audit line is a clean 20-second beat

If they ask

What does emergency lockdown actually do?
Every request from a non-admin account is rejected with HTTP 423 Locked until it is lifted. It is meant for a live incident or a suspected breach where you want the station's own administrators still able to operate while everything else is frozen. It requires a two-step confirm to enable, deliberately — it is a bigger hammer than any other control in the platform.
Why does every login attempt show the same IP address?
Because nginx proxies the API and the backend reads the connection's client address, which is the proxy container. nginx does set X-Real-IP but the backend is not consuming it. That is cosmetically wrong on this table, and functionally significant one layer down: the rate limiter keys on the same address, so every browser in the world shares one 120-requests-per-minute bucket. It is a small fix — trust the forwarded header — but it needs a backend restart, so it is not being changed tonight.
Is the backup safe to take while the system is running?
Yes — it is a WAL-safe SQLite snapshot rather than a file copy, so it does not capture a torn database mid-write. And the export itself is written to the audit log, so a handover is recorded rather than silent.

Careful / known limits

  • "Sign out all my sessions" on the security tab has NO confirmation dialog — one click revokes every token on the admin account and drops the presenter back to the login screen. It is positioned directly beneath the lockdown card, which is the thing the presenter most wants to point at on that tab. This is the highest-consequence single-click mistake available in the product. If the security tab is shown at all, point with the mouse well away from it.
  • "Activate lockdown" does have a two-step inline confirm, so it cannot fire accidentally — but if it is ever confirmed, every officer and citizen request returns HTTP 423 and the entire multi-role story collapses. Only click it if you intend to immediately cancel.
  • The users tab shows "QA Probe · qa_probe_i18n@example.com" — a test account created during last night's testing, visible on stage in the staff directory. The broadcast history shows "QA test advisory — ignore: Pre-demo check of the broadcast pipeline." Both are visible on the admin screens. There is no delete-user control in the UI to clean either up.
  • The coverage document I was given ENDS MID-SENTENCE inside this page, at "broadcast — send a city-wide advisory or disaster alert,". Everything after that point in the original testing run — including whatever pages followed Admin — is missing from what the presenter is holding. See the COVERAGE GAPS page.

  • 4 data exports, each audit-logged
  • 1 confirmation guard on lockdown; 0 confirmation guards on sign-out-all

Admin

Six admin tabs of very uneven quality: monitoring, data and broadcast are genuinely demo-worthy; users and teams are thin but honest; security is the most impressive one to TALK about and the most dangerous one to click.

Get there: Sign in as admin@city.gov / admin123 → top nav "Admin" (only visible to role=admin) → six tabs: users / teams / broadcast / security / monitoring / data.

Show this

  1. monitoring — a live ops panel polling every 5s: CPU, RAM, disk, DB size, uptime, total requests served, 5xx error count, indexed frame count, and green/grey chips for CLIP / YOLO / Face model load state plus an Operational badge.
    Do: Admin → monitoring. Leave it open for 10 seconds so the values tick.
    "Errors (5xx): 0" next to "Requests: 4,903" and three loaded models answers the reliability question before a judge asks it. It is real psutil data, not a mock — the numbers move while you watch.
  2. data — four one-click exports that actually download: a WAL-safe SQLite backup zip, cases.csv, complaints.csv and audit.csv. Every export is itself written to the audit log.
    Do: Admin → data → click Download on "Full backup (.zip)" → then Admin → security and point at the fresh "export · backup.zip" audit line.
    It is the answer to "what happens to our data / can we hand this over?" — measured 377 KB backup in 0.86s and a 302 KB complaints CSV in 0.37s, and the security tab then shows the download you just did as an audited event.
  3. broadcast — send a city-wide advisory or disaster alert, optionally scoped to one of 30 Ahmedabad areas, with severity and an expiry; it returns the recipient count, pins a banner for every logged-in user, and can be deactivated from the history list.
    Do: Admin → broadcast → Title + Message → Broadcast → then click "deactivate" on the row so it does not stay pinned.
    Measured "Sent to 5 users." in 3.06s, appears immediately in Recent broadcasts, one click to deactivate. It is the one admin action with a visible effect on every other screen.
  4. security — emergency lockdown (everything except admins gets HTTP 423), revoke-all-my-sessions, a login-attempt table with IP and result, and the full audit stream.
    The lockdown is a real middleware kill-switch, and the login table shows failed attempts, so it answers the breach-response question. Talk about it; see the warnings before clicking it.
  5. teams — expand "Cyber Investigation Unit" to see its 2 members with badge numbers, role pills and a lead star, and add/remove staff inline.
    Do: Admin → teams → click the team row to expand.
    Modest, but it grounds the RBAC story: the officer sees cases at all because he is in this team.

If they ask

Which of these six tabs are worth opening in front of judges?
monitoring (live CPU/RAM/DB/requests/errors + model load state), data (four exports that really download and are audited), broadcast (visible platform-wide effect), teams (grounds the RBAC story). users is a thin 5-row list with a form that crashes on invalid input. security is worth describing but its two buttons are both destructive.
Is the lockdown switch real?
Yes — LockdownMiddleware is in the request path. When the flag is on, everything except /api/health, /api/auth/login, /api/admin/* and requests carrying a valid admin JWT is rejected with HTTP 423 "CityShield is in emergency lockdown". I deliberately did NOT enable it during testing because other people were on the same stack; the state is persisted in app_settings with a 2s cache, and lifting it is one click.
What does 'Sign out all my sessions' actually do?
Increments the user's token_version, which invalidates every JWT ever issued to that account — including the one in the presenter's browser. You will be dropped to the login screen mid-demo. Same mechanism fires automatically when an admin changes someone's role or deactivates them, which is the correct behaviour but makes this button a bad thing to click on stage.
Every login row shows the same IP, 172.20.0.3 — is that real?
That is the nginx container's address. nginx forwards X-Real-IP but the backend logs request.client.host, so browser logins all collapse to one internal IP. The same shortcut means the 120 req/min rate limiter buckets every browser user together — which is why testing agents kept hitting 429. Both fix in one place: trust X-Forwarded-For behind the proxy.
Is the audit log complete enough to hand to an auditor?
It covers case lifecycle (create/close/evidence/visibility/assign/rate), complaint triage, user creation and updates, and every data export, each with actor id and timestamp — 57 rows currently, and exports show up in it within seconds. Gaps: reads are not audited, a complaint→case conversion is logged against the complaint rather than the new case, and there is no tamper-evidence (it is an ordinary SQLite table an admin could edit).
Can a non-admin reach the admin API by guessing URLs?
No. GET /api/users, GET /api/admin/system, GET /api/admin/security-events, GET /api/admin/export/*, POST /api/users, POST /api/teams and POST /api/notifications/broadcast all return 403 "Insufficient permissions" for lead, officer and citizen tokens — I ran all of them. GET /api/teams is deliberately open to any signed-in user (the team picker needs it).
Only one team and five users — is the RBAC just hard-coded?
No, it is data-driven: roles are a column on users, team membership is a foreign key, and case access is computed from assigned_team_id plus the case_assignments table. It is a small dataset, not a small mechanism — adding a second team through Admin → teams and moving the officer would immediately change what he can see.

Careful / known limits

  • Admin → users → "Add staff user": submitting with ANY invalid field (blank form, malformed email, password under 6 characters) crashes the entire React app to the "Something went wrong" error boundary and needs a page reload. Root cause is one line — the 422 body's `detail` is an array of pydantic error objects and CreateUserModal renders it directly as a React child (frontend/src/components/platform/AdminView.jsx, the setErr(e?.response?.data?.detail ...) call in CreateUserModal). A one-line coercion to a string fixes it, but it needs a frontend image rebuild, which I did not do because other agents are on this stack. Until then: do not open that form on stage.
  • Admin → security → "Sign out all my sessions" will log the presenter out instantly. Do not click.
  • Admin → security → "Activate lockdown" blocks every non-admin request platform-wide with HTTP 423. Safe to describe, risky to click; if clicked, "Lift lockdown" restores service immediately.
  • Admin → users is thin: 5 accounts including "QA Probe", a citizen created by another testing agent. There is no delete or deactivate control, and no role-change control in the UI (the API supports PATCH role).
  • Admin → teams has exactly 1 team with 2 members. Expanding it is fine; do not click the X next to Const. A. Patel — removing him from the team empties his case list and breaks the RBAC demo.
  • Recent broadcasts was empty before my test and now contains one deactivated row titled "QA test advisory — ignore". Delete-broadcast does not exist, so that row will be visible in the history panel during the demo.
  • Login-attempt IPs are all 172.20.0.3 (the nginx container), not real client addresses.
  • Shared 120 req/min rate limit: with a couple of browser tabs plus the 5s monitoring poller, the API starts returning 429 and screens silently render empty (the officer's case list showed 0 cases during one rate-limited run when the API had 10). Close spare tabs before the demo.

RBAC — role matrix

Four roles, and the difference is enforced in the API, not the CSS — measured 403s on 11 endpoints plus row-level filtering of evidence and a server-side one-message-per-case cap on citizens.

Get there: Sign out (avatar menu) and sign in as each account: admin@city.gov/admin123, lead@city.gov/lead123, officer@city.gov/officer123, citizen@example.com/citizen123. Compare the top nav and the same case in each.

Show this

  1. Open the SAME case in two browsers — admin and citizen — side by side. Admin sees 5 tabs and 5 evidence rows; the citizen sees 2 tabs and 1 evidence row, because the API returns one row for that token.
    Do: Browser A: admin → Cases → #33 → Evidence. Browser B (incognito): citizen@example.com / citizen123 → Cases → #33 → Evidence.
    This is the demo that answers "is it really role-based?" without opening a terminal: the difference is in the payload, not the stylesheet.
  2. The nav itself is computed from the role: admin gets 10 modules, lead and officer get 9 (no Admin), the citizen gets 3 (Legal Feed, Cases, Complaints) and the case heading changes to "My Cases".
    The citizen portal and the police console are the same React app and the same deployment — one login decides which product you are looking at.
  3. If a judge asks you to prove it, hit the API live: the officer token gets 403 "Insufficient permissions" on POST /api/users but 200 on POST /api/cases/31/evidence. Same token, two different answers, decided by require_role().
    Do: Log in as officer via POST /api/auth/login, take the token, then POST /api/users with it — 403 — and POST /api/cases/31/evidence with it — 200.
    It converts a UI claim into a verifiable HTTP status in five seconds.
  4. Citizens are capped at one message per case, server-side, with a human-readable refusal.
    It shows the API is doing policy, not just authentication: POST a second message and you get 403 "Citizens may send only one message per case".

Exact inputs

FieldValue — click to copyWhat you should get
Two-browser RBAC demoWindow 1: admin@city.gov / admin123. Window 2 (incognito): citizen@example.com / citizen123. Both open case #33.Admin: 5 tabs, 4 evidence rows, verdict, visibility toggle, Close button. Citizen: 2 tabs, only the citizen-tagged evidence row, verdict, star rating box.
Officer login for the 'can't close' beatofficer@city.gov / officer123 → Cases → any active caseSame 5 tabs as the lead, can add evidence and flip citizen visibility, but there is no Close case button and no New case button.
Lead login for the triage beatlead@city.gov / lead123 → Complaints1991 "Triage & open case" buttons. The officer sees zero.

Repeat with the admin token and it prints 200.

If they ask

Is this actually role-based access control, or just hidden buttons?
Both layers exist, and the server layer is the real one. Measured with real tokens: officer POST /api/users → 403, POST /api/cases → 403, POST /api/cases/31/close → 403, POST /api/complaints/{id}/triage → 403, GET /api/admin/system → 403, GET /api/admin/export/cases.csv → 403. Citizen additionally: GET /api/cases/31/timeline → 403 "Staff only", GET /api/analytics/summary → 403, POST evidence → 403, and a case they do not own → 403 "No access to this case". The UI hiding is convenience; removing it would change nothing about what the API permits.
Give me one concrete forbidden call and its status code.
POST /api/users with the officer's JWT returns HTTP 403 with body {"detail":"Insufficient permissions"}. The same call with the admin's JWT returns 200. It is enforced by a FastAPI dependency, require_role("admin"), on the route itself — not by the router and not by the frontend.
How are the four roles ranked?
citizen < officer < lead < admin, compared by role_rank(). Endpoints declare a minimum: evidence and citizen-visibility need officer; case creation, closing and complaint triage need lead; user/team management, exports, system status, security events and broadcasts need admin. Reads on a case additionally pass through can_access_case(), which checks team membership, personal assignment, or complainant ownership plus the citizen-visible flag.
Can an officer from another station read this station's cases?
No — the case query filters on assigned_team_id or an explicit row in case_assignments. There is only one team in this demo database, so you have to take the query on trust or create a second team live in Admin → teams to show it.
What stops a citizen from spamming an investigating officer?
A server-side cap of one message per case: the second POST returns 403 "Citizens may send only one message per case". The UI even changes the placeholder to "One message allowed…".
If you demote someone, do their existing sessions keep working?
No. PATCH /api/users/{id} bumps that user's token_version whenever role or active changes, and every JWT carries the version it was minted with, so all outstanding tokens for that user stop validating immediately. The same mechanism backs the "Sign out all my sessions" button.
Where is authorisation implemented — could a new endpoint forget it?
Yes, it could, and that is the honest weakness: it is per-route dependencies (require_role / get_current_user + get_case_or_403), not a global default-deny policy. A newly added route with no dependency would be open to any signed-in user.
Are the demo passwords the real security model?
No — admin123 / officer123 exist because judges need to log in in five seconds. Passwords are bcrypt-hashed, sessions are HS256 JWTs with an expiry and a token_version, failed logins are rate-limited to 10/min per IP and recorded with IP and outcome in the security tab. The weak passwords are demo seed data, gated by a setting.

Careful / known limits

  • The citizen account owns all 2001 complaints in this database, so "My complaints" shows 2001 rows. That is seed design, not an access-control failure — but do not open that screen while saying "a citizen only sees their own complaints", because it looks exactly like a leak.
  • GET /api/teams is readable by every signed-in role including citizens (it returns team name, station and member count). Deliberate — the team dropdowns need it — but it is the one endpoint that does not follow the pattern.
  • Authorisation is per-route, so a future endpoint added without a require_role dependency would be open to any authenticated user. There is no global default-deny.
  • Officer and lead have identical navigation, so the officer-vs-lead difference only becomes visible inside a case (no Close button) or on Complaints (no Triage button). Point at those two specifically or the distinction is invisible.
  • Case #38 is assigned to the officer (Const. A. Patel) but not to the lead's team, so the officer sees 10 cases while the lead sees 9. That asymmetry is my test artefact, not a bug — but if a judge counts cards across two logins, that is why the numbers differ.
  • During testing, the admin account's UI language was flipped to Hindi by another agent (it is a stored per-user preference, so it follows the account across browsers). If the interface comes up in Devanagari, switch it back with the language selector in the header before the demo starts.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • 4 roles seeded: admin, lead, officer, citizen (5 user accounts including one created by another test agent)
  • 11 endpoints probed per role; 403 "Insufficient permissions" returned on every admin-only and lead-only endpoint for officer and citizen tokens
  • Case visibility by role: admin 10, lead 9, officer 10, citizen 6
  • Evidence visibility on case #31: 5 rows for staff, 1 row for the citizen
  • Nav modules by role: admin 10, lead 9, officer 9, citizen 3
  • Citizen message cap: 1 per case, enforced with HTTP 403
  • Login rate limit: 10 attempts/min per IP; failed and successful attempts both logged with IP and timestamp

Language switching (English / हिन्दी / ગુજરાતી)

A per-user interface language stored on the account, covering 853 keys at exact three-way parity, that follows the user across logout, login and machines — with the CLIP search box deliberately left English-only and saying so in the user's own language.

Get there: Any screen → header language button (globe/Languages icon, right of the nav, shows the current language's native name) → pick English / हिन्दी / ગુજરાતી

Show this

  1. Switch to हिन्दी and the entire shell re-renders — nav, sidebar, filters, empty states, footer, helplines, legal pages — with no reload and no flash of English.
    Do: Log in as admin@city.gov / admin123 → click the language button in the header (it reads 'English') → choose हिन्दी → click through Dashboard, VisionScan, Live Alerts, Arbiter, Legal Feed, Cases, Complaints, Admin.
    853 keys per language at exact parity (en 853 / hi 853 / gu 853, zero missing, zero extra — verified by walking every section file). This is not a token demo language; the Anomaly Watch blurb, the Arbiter offline notice, the GovIntel facet counts and the 8 helpline labels are all translated.
  2. Log out while in Hindi — the LOGIN SCREEN is already Hindi. Log back in — still Hindi. Now log in as the citizen on the same browser — it comes up English, because that is the citizen's own saved preference.
    Do: Switch to हिन्दी → sign out (icon at far right of header) → observe the Hindi login screen → sign back in as admin → still Hindi → sign out → sign in as citizen@example.com / citizen123 → English.
    Proves the setting is genuinely per-user and server-side, not a browser toggle. Two officers sharing one terminal each get their own language. The login screen trick uses localStorage so the very first screen is already localised before anyone has authenticated.
  3. In a fully Hindi UI, the VisionScan search placeholder reads 'अंग्रेज़ी में लिखें, जैसे "person in red jacket near the gate"' — the app tells the operator, in Hindi, to type the query in English.
    Do: In Hindi, go to विज़नस्कैन → read the search placeholder aloud → type 'red car' → 1 correct match on CAM-14. (Do not type Hindi into the search box on stage; it returns noise.)
    The CLIP text encoder is English-only. Rather than silently returning garbage for a Hindi query, the UI surfaces the model's real boundary in the operator's own language. It is a designed limitation, not a gap — and I measured the difference: 'red car' returns 1 precise hit on CAM-14 (the red-car camera); 'लाल कार' returns 8 scattered 27%-confidence frames topped by CAM-09, which is pedestrians.
  4. Gujarati is equally complete — including the Legal Feed, where the UI chrome is Gujarati but the 33 government documents and 20+ department names stay in their published English.
    Do: Switch to ગુજરાતી → open કાનૂની ફીડ (Legal Feed) → the facets, counts and buttons are Gujarati; the document titles and departments are not.
    Shows the team drew the right line: interface strings translate, legal instruments and official department names do not, because a translated 'Central Board of Direct Taxes' would be wrong in a police record.
  5. Only three strings in the whole dictionary are byte-identical across all three languages: UPI, SMS, WhatsApp.
    Do: Quote the number if asked. It is verifiable from frontend/src/i18n/sections/*.js.
    A concrete, checkable answer to 'how do you know it's really translated?' — an automated parity check over every section file found exactly three, and all three are brand names that must not be translated.

If they ask

Is this real translation or machine output with English fallback?
Real, and checkable. There are 853 keys in each of en/hi/gu with zero missing and zero extra — I enumerated every one of the 16 section files and diffed them. Exactly three strings are byte-identical across all three languages: UPI, SMS and WhatsApp, which are brand names. There IS an English fallback in the lookup chain (active language → English → the key itself) but nothing is currently reaching it.
Why is the CCTV search box English-only when the rest of the UI is trilingual?
Because CLIP's text encoder is trained on English. We embed the query into the same vector space as the frames, so a Devanagari query lands in a meaningless region. I measured it: 'red car' returns one hit on CAM-14, the actual red-car camera; 'लाल कार' returns 8 hits at ~27% topped by CAM-09, which is pedestrians. Rather than hide that, the Hindi placeholder literally says 'अंग्रेज़ी में लिखें' — write in English. Fixing it properly means swapping in a multilingual CLIP variant or putting an IndicTrans layer in front of the encoder; both are real options, neither is done.
Is the language a browser setting or does it follow the officer?
It follows the officer. setLang writes to localStorage AND fires PATCH /api/auth/me/preferences, which persists language on the users row. I verified the whole loop: set Hindi, sign out, sign back in — still Hindi; then sign in as the citizen on the same browser and it comes up English, because that is the citizen's stored value. localStorage is used for exactly one thing: rendering the login screen in the last-used language before anyone has authenticated.
Show me a string that is still English when it shouldn't be.
Several, and they cluster on the VisionScan screen. The results header renders '1 matches for red car' in English — and the annoying part is that the translation key vision.matchesFor exists and IS translated in both languages; App.jsx:470 just hardcodes the word. Same story for the 'Find every object' button at App.jsx:510, where the identical key is used correctly two lines above. Plus all 16 camera tiles show 'Ready' because utils.js statusBadge() hardcodes the four status labels, and role badges show raw admin/lead/officer/citizen because enums.js has no role_* keys. None of these break anything; they are five or six one-line wiring misses, not missing translation work.
Why not use react-i18next?
Deliberate — there's a comment about it in the module. Three fixed languages, static strings, simple {placeholder} substitution: a React context plus three plain objects does it in about 80 lines instead of a 40KB dependency and its plugin chain. Nothing outside i18n/index.jsx knows how lookup works, so swapping in a real library later is a contained change. The trade-off they accepted is no plurals — which is exactly why you see '1 matches' instead of '1 match'.
What happens to a language that isn't one of the three?
The API rejects it: PATCH with {"language":"fr"} returns 400 'language must be one of en|hi|gu'. Client-side, setLang no-ops if the code isn't in the dictionary map. And any key that somehow missed a translation degrades to readable English rather than showing a raw key or a blank cell.
Do the AI-generated outputs (Arbiter FIR drafts, CrimeGPT documents) get translated too?
No, and that's a different problem from UI strings. The Arbiter statute corpus is IPC / IT Act 2000 / CrPC in English, and CrimeGPT's document generator takes a language argument separately. The UI chrome around them is fully Hindi/Gujarati; the legal text is not, because a machine-translated statute reference in a police document would be a liability rather than a feature.
If the app crashes, is the error screen translated?
No — 'Something went wrong' and 'Reload' are English-only. It's a class component, so it can't use the useT hook, and arguably you don't want the crash handler depending on the i18n context that may be the thing that broke. Honest answer: it's defensible, but it means a Hindi demo that hits an error shows an English wall.

Careful / known limits

  • i18n LEAK (most visible): the search results header renders '1 matches for red car' in English in every language — frontend/src/App.jsx:470 hardcodes 'matches for' even though the key vision.matchesFor exists and is translated (hi 'मिलान —', gu 'મેચ —'). This is the single most-looked-at line on the VisionScan screen.
  • i18n LEAK: the 'Find every object' nudge action button is hardcoded English at frontend/src/App.jsx:510, while the SAME key t('vision.findEveryObject') is used correctly two lines above at :504. In one Hindi screenshot you can see both — 'हर वस्तु खोजें' in the sentence and 'Find every object' on the button.
  • i18n LEAK: all 16 camera tiles show 'Ready' in English in Hindi and Gujarati. frontend/src/utils.js:18-31 statusBadge() returns hardcoded labels Ready / Processing / Queued / Error with no t() call. My first static scan missed this because it's a plain JS object, not JSX.
  • i18n LEAK: role labels render raw lowercase English (admin, lead, officer, citizen) at frontend/src/App.jsx:328 (header user chip) and frontend/src/components/platform/AdminView.jsx:60, :611, :631. enums.js has no role_* keys at all.
  • i18n LEAK: 'Scoped to …' and 'search all feeds' at frontend/src/App.jsx:439 and :445 stay English when a single camera is selected. Avoid selecting a single camera during a Hindi walkthrough.
  • i18n LEAK: the mobile feeds-drawer 'Close' button, frontend/src/App.jsx:397, is hardcoded although common.close is translated in all three languages. Only visible below the md breakpoint.
  • i18n LEAK: frontend/src/components/ErrorBoundary.jsx:28-41 — 'Something went wrong', the body paragraph and 'Reload' are English-only. Defensible (class component, no hook) but it is the screen the register-crash below lands on.
  • PLURALISATION: '1 matches for …' and '1 frames selected' — the hand-rolled i18n has no plural support. Cosmetic, but a judge reading the screen will notice.
  • SILENT SAVE FAILURE: setLang fires updatePreferences as fire-and-forget with .catch(() => {}) (frontend/src/i18n/index.jsx:55). When the API was rate-limited during my testing the PATCH was rejected and the language silently reverted on next login, with no indication to the user. Single-presenter demo won't hit this; two people testing simultaneously did.
  • SHARED-ACCOUNT GOTCHA: language is per USER, so if two people are signed into admin@city.gov at once they will fight over the setting — I watched it flip back to English mid-test because another session changed it. Make sure nobody else is logged in as admin during the demo. I have reset all four demo accounts to English.
  • There is no language switcher ON the login screen — it inherits the last language this browser used. If the demo laptop was last used in Gujarati, the sign-in page opens in Gujarati and there is no control to change it until after sign-in.

Mobile / field view (390×844)

The responsive field view works and never overflows horizontally — but the bottom tab bar renders as icons with NO text labels, because of a one-word bug.

Get there: Open the same URL on a phone-sized viewport. The desktop top-nav is hidden below md and replaced by a fixed bottom tab bar; every module renders in the same shell.

Show this

  1. The whole command dashboard reflows to a 390px phone with zero horizontal overflow — all 10 KPI tiles, the 14-day trend, all six breakdown charts, readable.
    Do: If a judge asks about field use, resize the browser narrow rather than promising a phone app. The bottom tab bar appears automatically below the md breakpoint.
    It is the difference between a control-room tool and a tool a constable can use at a scene. Measured: document.body.scrollWidth 390 = window.innerWidth 390 on the dashboard, on the login screen and after navigation — nothing spills.

Exact inputs

FieldValue — click to copyWhat you should get
Viewport to use if asked about field use390 × 844 (iPhone 14/15 size)Bottom tab bar appears, dashboard reflows, no horizontal scroll. The bar will be icons-only
Role to demo on mobileofficer@city.gov / officer1239 bottom-bar icons (Admin correctly absent). This is the role that would actually carry a phone
What to say about the missing labels if noticed"The mobile bar is icon-only in this build — the labels are in the translation catalogue but the bottom bar is reading the wrong key. One-word fix, not shipped tonight because it needs a frontend rebuild."Honest and correct. It is genuinely `{m.label}` where it should be `{t(m.labelKey)}`

If they ask

Is there a mobile app?
No, and we would not build one yet — this is the same React app reflowing responsively, which means a constable at a scene sees exactly the same data as the control room with no second codebase to keep in sync and no app-store deployment path to negotiate with a police IT department. The trade-off is that it needs a browser and a connection.
Why are the bottom tab icons unlabelled?
A bug, and a small one: the bottom bar renders a property that does not exist on the nav item, so it prints nothing, while the desktop nav correctly resolves the same item through the translation catalogue. The labels exist in all three languages already. It is a one-word change that needs a frontend rebuild, which we are not doing the night before a demo.

Careful / known limits

  • MOBILE BOTTOM TAB BAR HAS NO LABELS AT ALL — nine icon-only buttons on every screen, at every viewport below the md breakpoint. Verified by reading the innerText of every button in the fixed bottom nav: nine empty strings. The cause is one word in frontend/src/App.jsx — the bottom bar renders {m.label} where the nav items carry labelKey and the desktop nav renders {t(m.labelKey)}. It is a one-word fix but it requires a frontend rebuild, so I did not apply it. Practical impact: if the presenter shrinks the window on stage to show the field view, the navigation loses all its text. Either show mobile deliberately and own it, or do not resize the window below ~768px during the demo.
  • The mobile bug is completely silent — zero console errors, zero API errors — which is exactly why five agents of desktop testing did not surface it.
  • Only the dashboard was exercised deeply at mobile size; the heavier screens (City Map with Leaflet, VisionScan result grids) were not measured for overflow at 390px, so do not promise that every module is phone-perfect.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • Viewport 390×844: document.body.scrollWidth 390 = window.innerWidth 390 — no horizontal overflow on the dashboard
  • 9 bottom-nav buttons for the officer role (10 for admin), 100% of them rendering with empty label text
  • 0 uncaught page errors and 0 API responses ≥400 on mobile

25 of 27

App shell — language switcher, notification bell, alert ticker

The persistent chrome nobody in the coverage document exercised: a working three-language switch that translates the whole product (not just labels), a notification bell wired to real platform events, and an idle anomaly ticker.

Get there: Any signed-in screen. Top-right of the header, left to right: (VisionScan only) systems-ready chip, language pill, siren alert ticker, bell, user name/role, sign-out.

Show this

  1. Click the language pill, pick हिन्दी, and the ENTIRE dashboard re-renders in Hindi — nav, KPI tile labels, chart titles and the crime-category names: कमांड डैशबोर्ड / शिकायतें 2003 / अनुक्रमित फ़्रेम 417 / मारपीट 492 / चेन स्नैचिंग 214.
    Do: Language pill → हिन्दी. Let the dashboard repaint. Then → ગુજરાતી (ડેશબોર્ડ / ફરિયાદો / અનુક્રમિત…). Then back to English. Three clicks, about twelve seconds.
    It is not a nav-bar translation. The category taxonomy, the chart legends and the KPI captions all move, in under a second, offline, and the choice persists across a page reload because it is stored per user. For a Gujarat government buyer this is the difference between a demo and a deployable product.
  2. What deliberately does NOT translate is exactly right: locality names stay Latin ("Vatva, Ahmedabad 262") in Hindi mode.
    Place names, camera IDs and CLIP search queries are data and identifiers, not UI copy — translating them would break search and make evidence unciteable. The line to say is "we translate the interface, never the evidence".
  3. The notification bell is fed by real platform events, not a mock list: complaint updates quoting the officer's own response text, case-closed prompts to rate, case-shared, case-message, and city-wide advisories.
    On the citizen account the bell shows the loop closing in its own words: "Update on your complaint 'Online fraud reported': FIR registered; 1930 freeze request raised with the bank nodal officer." That is the exact text the officer typed in the triage modal, delivered to the citizen.

If they ask

Is the Hindi a machine translation done at request time?
No — it is a static per-language message catalogue shipped with the build, so it costs nothing at runtime and works offline. The switch is a per-user preference stored on the user record, which is why it survives a reload and why two officers on the same station can run the console in different languages.
Everything is translated except the place names. Why?
Because they are identifiers, not copy. Locality names, camera IDs, filenames and frame IDs get quoted into evidence and into the PDF report; translating them would break the link back to the source. Separately, the CLIP search queries must stay in English because the embedding model is English-trained — translating a query would silently degrade retrieval. So the rule is: interface translates, evidence and model inputs do not.
So can an officer type a search query in Hindi?
They can type it, but they should not expect good results — CLIP's text encoder is English-trained and a Devanagari query lands in a poorly-populated region of the embedding space. That is a known ceiling, not something the language switch fixes. The same limitation shows up in the PDF documents, where Indic body text is a separate unsolved problem in the renderer.
Does the notification bell push, or poll?
Polls, every 5 seconds, alongside the anomaly-summary poller. That polling is also part of why the shared rate-limit bucket drains so fast — the bell and the ticker together are two of the six background pollers running on every open tab.

Careful / known limits

  • The notification panel closes ONLY by clicking the bell a second time. Escape does not close it and clicking outside does not close it — verified. Worse, it stays open across a module change, so it will hang over the top-right corner of the next screen the presenter navigates to. If it is covering something, click the bell again.
  • Opening the bell marks everything read and the red count badge is gone for good. I burned the admin account's badge doing this test, so the admin bell now shows no unread count. If the presenter wants a red badge visible on stage, do not open the bell beforehand — or use the citizen account, which still has unread items.
  • The admin bell's three notifications are all QA artefacts: 'New complaint filed: QA probe scope test', 'New complaint filed: QA UPI OTP fraud 013203', and '[MEDIUM] QA test advisory — ignore: Pre-demo check of the broadcast pipeline.' If a judge asks to see the bell on the admin account, that is what they will read. The citizen bell is clean and much better.
  • The alert ticker is permanently grey because there are zero anomaly events. It is not broken, but do not point at it expecting a red pulse.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • 3 languages, switchable per user, persisted across reload — verified on the Command Dashboard
  • 10 nav labels + 10 KPI captions + 6 chart titles + 8 category names all translate to hi and gu
  • Locality labels deliberately untranslated — verified: 'Vatva, Ahmedabad 262' renders identically in Hindi mode
  • Admin bell: 3 notifications (2 of them QA rows). Citizen bell: 12 notifications across 5 event types
  • Bell and anomaly ticker both poll on a 5-second interval
  • 0 active broadcasts — GET /api/notifications/broadcasts/active returns []

404 / dead-end sweep — all 10 modules

A systematic click-through of all ten modules watching pageerror and every /api/ response ≥400 — all ten render clean when the box isn't under load, with a short list of screens that fail badly rather than gracefully when it is.

Get there: Signed in as admin@city.gov, clicked every module in the top nav in turn and watched for errors, infinite spinners and unexplained empty panels

Show this

  1. All 10 modules — Dashboard, VisionScan, Live Alerts, City Map, Arbiter, CrimeGPT, Legal Feed, Cases, Complaints, Admin — render with zero JavaScript errors and zero HTTP 4xx/5xx.
    Do: Click each of the ten nav items in order. Watch the browser console stay empty.
    Nothing 404s, nothing throws, nothing silently spins. A judge can click anywhere in the top nav without the presenter having to steer them away from a broken screen.
  2. The empty states are written, not blank. Live Alerts with no incidents says 'All clear — no active alerts.' under an explanation of what Anomaly Watch is watching for; Arbiter says 'Describe an incident or ask a question…'; Cases with a filter that matches nothing explains itself.
    Do: Open Live Alerts (usually empty) — the screen reads as intentional, not broken.
    Empty panels with no explanation are the classic demo failure. Every module I hit with no data told me why it was empty and what to do next — and all of them are translated into Hindi and Gujarati too.
  3. The Command Dashboard is fully populated on landing: 2003 complaints, 4 open cases, 6 closed, 115h average resolve time, 4.17/5 average citizen rating, 30 evidence items, 16 feeds, 417 indexed frames, 5 users — plus a 14-day complaint histogram, a case-status donut, category and status breakdowns, and top complaint locations.
    Do: Log in as admin — this is the landing screen. Nothing to click.
    The first screen after login already answers 'is there real data behind this?' with eleven populated tiles and five charts, and it refreshes every 15 seconds.

If they ask

Did anything 404 or throw?
No. I clicked all ten modules as admin while listening for JavaScript exceptions and for every /api/ response of 400 or above. Zero of each, and zero infinite spinners. The one caveat is that this was on a healthy box — under rate limiting the picture changes, which is the next question.
What happens when the backend can't keep up?
That's where the sharp edges are. There's a token-bucket limiter at 120 requests/min per IP for general API calls, and during testing several agents were driving the same box, so I got to watch the degraded path. Dashboard and Legal Feed render a bare 'Too many requests' with no retry affordance. City Map is worse — it sits on 'City data loading…' indefinitely with no error state at all, so a failed fetch is indistinguishable from a slow one. And because auth.jsx clears the token on any /auth/me failure, a page reload during a burst logs you out entirely. For one presenter on one laptop none of this fires; it's a warning about what happens if judges start clicking on their own devices.
Why does the complaint inbox have 2002 rows and no pagination?
The synthetic seed lays down a high-volume incident stream so the map, analytics and backtest have something real to compute over — 2002 complaints across 8 categories and 20+ Ahmedabad areas. The inbox renders all of them: 29,022 DOM nodes and 1,993 triage buttons. It's not broken — 1.7 seconds to render, 17.4 MB of heap — but there's no pagination, no search and no filter, so finding a specific complaint means scrolling. Don't go looking for one live; open it, show the top few rows, and move on.
Which screen would you keep a judge away from?
None of the ten, on the merits — they all render. What I'd avoid is the registration form with a short password, which white-screens the whole app, and scrolling the complaint inbox. I'd also not reload the page mid-demo if the box feels slow, because of the logout bug.
Is the empty Live Alerts screen a failure?
No, and it's worth saying out loud rather than clicking past. Anomaly Watch runs a hybrid CLIP+YOLO detector over the feeds for fire, smoke, accidents, weapons and violence, and pins incidents the moment they're seen. With the seeded clips there's nothing currently active, so it correctly says 'All clear — no active alerts.' The filters and the explanation are still there. An empty alerts board in a policing product is the good outcome.

Careful / known limits

  • City Map has NO error state. On any failed fetch it displays 'City data loading…' forever — no error, no retry, no timeout. I observed this repeatedly while the API was rate-limited. A judge watching that screen has no way to tell a dead request from a slow one.
  • Dashboard and Legal Feed degrade to a bare 'Too many requests' string with no retry button when rate-limited. Functional but ugly, and it looks like a crash to anyone who doesn't know what a 429 is.
  • Page reload while the API is busy logs you out: frontend/src/auth.jsx:16-18 clears the auth token on ANY /auth/me failure including a 429, not just a 401. Avoid reloading the page mid-demo.
  • Complaints inbox renders all 2002 rows with no pagination, no search and no filter — 29,022 DOM nodes, 1,993 triage buttons, 1.7s render, 2.6s to scroll to the bottom. Show the top of the list; don't hunt for a specific complaint on stage.
  • Not a module fault, but it will end the demo if hit: registration (and Admin → Add staff user) with a password under 6 characters white-screens the entire app. See the Login page.
  • The first VisionScan search after a cold start loads CLIP and takes 20-30 seconds. Warm it with one throwaway 'red car' search before going on stage or the flagship feature opens with half a minute of silence.

Measured numbers you can quote

  • 10/10 modules render with 0 JavaScript errors and 0 HTTP 4xx/5xx on a healthy box
  • Command Dashboard tiles: 2003 complaints, 4 open cases, 6 closed, 115h avg resolve, 4.17/5 avg rating (6 ratings), 30 evidence, 16 feeds, 417 indexed frames, 10 total cases, 5 users
  • Complaints module: 351,527 characters of rendered text, 29,022 DOM nodes, 1,993 triage buttons, 1.7s render, 2.6s scroll-to-end, 17.4 MB JS heap
  • Legal Feed: 33 indexed documents — 27 central, 6 Gujarat; facets across GR (2), notification (7), circular (2), act (5), rule (2), judgment (2), scheme (4)
  • Arbiter: offline mode, 25 statute sections
  • Live Alerts: 5 detection types (fire, smoke, accident, weapon, violence), 3 status filters
  • Admin: 6 sub-tabs (users, teams, broadcast, security, monitoring, data)
  • Rate limits that shape the degraded path: 120 general API req/min, 10 auth req/min, 20 uploads/min per IP
  • Warm search latency 0.1s API / ~5s through the UI; cold first search 20-30s (CLIP load)

COVERAGE GAPS AND RISKS

What is missing from your test report, which of its claims no longer hold, and the one thing most likely to break on stage.

Get there: Read this first. It is the delta between the coverage document you are holding and the live app at 02:15 IST on 20 August 2026, about seven hours before you present.

Exact inputs

FieldValue — click to copyWhat you should get
The one-minute fix for the zero trend barAfter 05:30 IST, file ONE complaint through the citizen portal (or triage one) before the judges arriveA complaint created at 09:00 IST is stamped 03:30 UTC on 20 Aug and lands in the newest bucket, so the last bar renders with a real value instead of a 1px stub. This is far safer than shifting rows or re-seeding, and the report's option list did not include it. Do NOT run the seed with force — it INSERTs again and would double the stream
Rate-limit hygiene before you startExactly ONE browser tab. Close every other tab pointed at the app, on every machine. Stop any background agents.One idle tab burns 66–74 requests/minute of a 120/minute shared budget. Two tabs 429 continuously
Rate-limit hygiene during the demoKeep "Grouping moments" ON for every VisionScan searchGrouped "red car" requests 24 thumbnails; ungrouped requests 60 and will black out most of the grid
