If they ask

What is the single biggest risk of something breaking live tomorrow?
The shared rate limiter, and it is not close. I reproduced it: 150 sequential thumbnail requests trip HTTP 429 at request #124, in 2.38 seconds. /thumbnails/ falls into the same 120-requests-per-minute bucket as the API, and because nginx does not forward the client IP, EVERY browser shares ONE bucket — which you can see with your own eyes on Admin → security, where every login attempt logs IP 172.20.0.3. One idle tab already spends 66–74 of those 120 requests per minute on background polling. So a single ungrouped 60-card search grid is enough to break the demo, and the failure modes are ugly and silent: thumbnails render as black rectangles with bounding boxes floating on them, the entire Command Dashboard is replaced by one red 'Too many requests' box, the City Map hangs forever on 'Loading city intelligence…' with no error and no retry, CrimeGPT shows 'no open cases', and the Cases timeline shows 'No activity.' None of those look like rate limiting — they look like a broken product. The behavioural mitigations are: one tab only, grouping always ON, and if a screen goes blank, wait 30 seconds and let the poller heal it rather than clicking again.
Is any headline item actually unimpressive or risky to show on stage?
Four. (1) LIVE ALERTS. The headline is an architecture argument delivered over an empty screen. 'Zero false alarms on 417 frames' is a legitimate and honest claim, but you are asking judges to be impressed by a blank list — and the presenter cannot show a single detection. Either ingest a fire clip beforehand and accept that FEEDS goes 16→17, or move this screen to a 20-second architectural aside and do not build a beat around it. (2) SUSPECT FACE with grouping OFF. The report suggests toggling grouping off for a fuller 13-card grid; I measured the ungrouped tail and the weakest of the 13 scores 0.3247, barely over the 0.30 accept threshold. A grid whose last cards read 32% invites exactly the question you do not want. Keep grouping ON and open the gallery instead. (3) CASES BY SEVERITY on the dashboard. The report calls it a single 'High 9' bar that looks broken; it now has two bars (Critical 1, High 9), which is better but still the thinnest chart on a screen full of good ones. Skip it either way. (4) The PER-OBJECT 'red car' story, which is the strongest 20 seconds in VisionScan, has a trap directly attached: the frame-count badge promises 32 frames and the detail modal shows one image with no gallery. Tell the red-car story from the result grid, and tell the gallery story from a YOLO chip or the face search.
Are any claims in the report implausible or contradictory?
None were fabricated — I spot-checked the ones I doubted most and every single one held: the 24-vs-38 scoping inflation, the 13 hits for 'elephant in a swimming pool', the 0.86 reference-image match, the 0.8428 face match on CAM-07 with zero cross-camera hits, the IT Act 66F cyber-terrorism landmine, and the ungated zone/line endpoints. Two things drifted rather than being wrong: the backtest numbers moved slightly (HR@10 0.7786 not 0.7818, PAI@10 2.336 not 2.345) as the synthetic window slid, and the dashboard KPIs moved again overnight. One claim is now conservative in your favour: text search measured 37–48 ms tonight, not the reported 80 ms median. The one contradiction I found is between the report and the live data, not between agents: the Complaints page tells you to triage 'the top of the list', and the top of the list is now a QA test complaint.
What did nobody test?
Judging by what I found empty: the mobile/field view (I found a real bug there in five minutes), the Live Feed tab and the whole live-stream ingestion path, the Delete-feed buttons, the notification-panel close behaviour, and the sign-out-all confirmation gap. But the bigger answer is that your DOCUMENT is incomplete rather than your testing. The screenshot directory contains 269 files including 37 i18n shots, 13 RBAC shots, 17 login shots, 10 citizen shots and 11 'sweep' shots — substantial test runs whose written reports never reached me. The document you are holding stops mid-sentence inside the Admin page. Find the rest of it before you rely on it as your script.

Cause is one word in frontend/src/App.jsx: the bottom bar renders {m.label} while the nav items carry labelKey and the desktop nav correctly renders {t(m.labelKey)}. One-word fix, needs a frontend rebuild, NOT worth doing tonight. Practical rule: do not shrink the browser window below ~768px on stage unless you intend to show and own the field view.
  • 8. THE LIVE FEED TAB IS AN UNTESTED HEADLINE FEATURE AND A FORCED CHOICE. VisionScan → Camera Feeds → 'Live Feed' offers RTSP/HLS/YouTube-live ingestion with three pre-wired public streams (all three verified reachable from inside the backend container tonight) and a printed ethics warning worth reading aloud. But both 'Go Live' and 'Capture once' create a 17th feed, and so does ingesting a fire clip for Live Alerts. You can have AT MOST ONE of: the round '16 feeds / 417 frames' dashboard numbers, a live-stream demo, or a non-empty Live Alerts screen. Decide at breakfast, not on stage. It also needs venue internet, unlike everything else in the platform.

Admin → broadcast history shows 'QA test advisory — ignore: Pre-demo check of the broadcast pipeline.' The admin notification bell shows two 'QA probe' complaint alerts. The citizen bell shows the QA advisory second from the top. None of it can be deleted from the UI. It is all plausible-looking, so the safest play is simply not to open the admin bell or the broadcast history.

VERIFIED-STILL-TRUE landmines from your report, so you can trust these: /api/videos/{id}/zone-analytics and /line-crossings return 200 with NO auth token (both confirmed); per-object search cannot return an empty result ('elephant in a swimming pool' → 13 hits, confirmed); camera scoping can inflate hit counts (red car: 24 unscoped, 38 scoped to CAM-12, confirmed); and Legal Q&A on WhatsApp threats returns IT Act 66F Cyber terrorism — life imprisonment — as its second citation (confirmed, do not run it).
